I mean, I get tired after work like anyone would, but I haven’t really paid close attention to whether it’s more than normal or happening when I haven’t done much.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it hard to focus or concentrate on tasks, like following instructions or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s actually one of the reasons I came in. My head just doesn’t seem to stay on task anymore. Like at work, I’ll be doing inventory and lose track of what I was counting, or I’ll go to the supply closet and forget what I went there for. It’s like this heavy, flat feeling that just kind of follows me around. Even when I’m at work pushing the cleaning cart down an empty hallway, or at home sitting with my family trying to act normal, it’s still there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous, anxious, or on edge about things in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really nervous, I guess. I mean, I worry about regular stuff sometimes, but I haven’t noticed feeling on edge all the time or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a loss of pleasure or interest in activities that you used to enjoy, like hobbies, time with family, or other things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I still do some of the usual stuff, but I haven’t paid close attention to whether I’m enjoying it less. It’s more just… I feel kind of flat a lot of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or low on energy a lot of the time, even when you haven't been doing much?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I get tired after work like anyone would, but I haven’t really paid close attention to whether it’s more than normal or happening when I haven’t done much.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it hard to focus or concentrate on tasks, like following instructions or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s actually one of the reasons I came in. My head just doesn’t seem to stay on task anymore. Like at work, I’ll be doing inventory and lose track of what I was counting, or I’ll go to the supply closet and forget what I went there for. And when I try studying for the supervisor certification, I keep reading the same line over and over without it sticking.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in most activities, even ones you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say “most activities.” I haven’t really been tracking it like that. I still do things, but I guess I’m not always sure if I’m enjoying them or just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in most activities, even ones you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m still not sure I’d put it as “most activities.” I guess some things don’t feel like much anymore, but I haven’t really noticed enough to say that for sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I guess I just don’t pay close attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you noticed that things you used to really enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t seem to give you the same pleasure anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t noticed a big difference like that. I still do some of the same things, but I guess I don’t pay much attention to whether they feel as good as they used to.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you say that you’ve stopped getting pleasure or interest from things you used to really enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that, no. I mean, I’m not sure I’m getting a lot out of things, but I haven’t really noticed that I’ve stopped enjoying them or lost interest completely.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past several weeks, would you say that you’ve stopped getting pleasure or interest from things you used to really enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends?\n\nPatient: I’m still not really sure. I don’t think I can say I’ve stopped getting pleasure from things. I do them, and some days they’re okay, but I haven’t noticed a clear change like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that even simple tasks take a lot more mental effort than they used to, or that you're struggling to focus even when you're not distracted?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s actually a big part of why I came in. My head just doesn’t seem to stay on task like it used to. I’ll be doing inventory at work and lose track of what I was counting, or I’ll go to the supply closet and forget what I went in there for. Even when I’m studying for the supervisor certification, I keep reading the same line over and over and it just doesn’t stick.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at the past few months, have you noticed that you no longer look forward to or enjoy activities that you used to care about, even if you still do them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly. I still do the things I usually do, and sometimes they’re fine. I haven’t really noticed that I don’t look forward to them anymore or that I can’t enjoy them. It’s more hard for me to say than a clear yes.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say that you’ve lost interest or stopped getting pleasure from activities you used to really care about, even if you still do them sometimes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a clear way. I still do those things sometimes, and I can still get something out of them. It’s not like I’ve just stopped caring or nothing feels good anymore. I’m just not sure I’d say that’s been the issue.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’ve also been trying to study for a supervisor certification, but I keep reading the same line over and over and it just doesn’t stick. It’s been getting frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days I do feel pretty down. It’s like this heavy, flat feeling that just kind of follows me around. Even when I’m at work pushing the cleaning cart down an empty hallway, or at home sitting with my family trying to act normal, it’s still there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous, anxious, or on edge about things in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really nervous, I guess. I mean, I worry about regular stuff sometimes, but I haven’t noticed feeling on edge all the time or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a loss of pleasure or interest in activities that you used to enjoy, like hobbies, time with family, or other things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I still do some of the usual stuff, but I haven’t paid close attention to whether I’m enjoying it less. It’s more just… I feel kind of flat a lot of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or low on energy a lot of the time, even when you haven't been doing much?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I get tired after work like anyone would, but I haven’t really paid close attention to whether it’s more than normal or happening when I haven’t done much.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it hard to focus or concentrate on tasks, like following instructions or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s actually one of the reasons I came in. My head just doesn’t seem to stay on task anymore. Like at work, I’ll be doing inventory and lose track of what I was counting, or I’ll go to the supply closet and forget what I went there for. And when I try studying for the supervisor certification, I keep reading the same line over and over without it sticking.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in most activities, even ones you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say “most activities.” I haven’t really been tracking it like that. I still do things, but I guess I’m not always sure if I’m enjoying them or just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in most activities, even ones you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m still not sure I’d put it as “most activities.” I guess some things don’t feel like much anymore, but I haven’t really noticed enough to say that for sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to the last week or two—have you noticed that activities you used to look forward to, like hobbies or time with friends, feel less enjoyable or even uninteresting now?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed a clear change like that in the last week or two. I still do the usual stuff, but I guess I don’t always think about whether I’m enjoying it or not.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past few weeks, have you found that activities you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends, have become less pleasurable or interesting to you?\n\nPatient: I can’t really say for sure. I’ve still been doing some of the same things, but I haven’t noticed a clear shift where they feel less enjoyable. I guess I just don’t pay close attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you noticed that things you used to really enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t seem to give you the same pleasure anymore?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t noticed a big difference like that. I still do some of the same things, but I guess I don’t pay much attention to whether they feel as good as they used to.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you say that you’ve stopped getting pleasure or interest from things you used to really enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that, no. Even when I’m studying for the supervisor certification, I keep reading the same line over and over and it just doesn’t stick.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at the past few months, have you noticed that you no longer look forward to or enjoy activities that you used to care about, even if you still do them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly.
